| **A signed-in seeker's recent searches follow the account, not the browser** (D248) — the "pick up where you left off" rail lived under `dzRecentSearches:<mobile>`, a key that *promises* per-account continuity from the one place that cannot deliver it: the search run on a phone at lunch was simply absent on the laptop that evening, and clearing site data threw the lot away. `GET`/`PUT /me/recent-searches` now own the rail, and with it the three rules the client used to guess at — the six-row cap, the server timestamp, and dedupe by the **normalised URL rather than the label**, since two searches that print the same words can carry different filters and collapsing them loses one. Every assertion is one a mock provider cannot satisfy, because it writes the same browser key the old code did: the `PUT` is caught on the wire, the row is re-read straight from the API, and a **second browser context** for the same account finds it in both readers — the Home chip rail and the dashboard's `resume-search` card. The cap test asserts the whole ordered set rather than just "the oldest is gone", because an over-eager eviction that kept only the newest would satisfy the absence on its own. The last test is the boundary: an anonymous visitor's identical search still lands in the local rail (the positive anchor) and issues **no request to the endpoint at all** — there is no account to attach it to, and opening a server-side browsing log for someone who never signed in is a cost with no benefit. URL allow-listing and the 422s for absolute, foreign and overlong URLs stay in `RecentSearchTest`, where a rejected write leaves nothing behind | consumer/account | recent-searches | ✅ |
| `/dashboard#enquiries` flatmate host inbox — host creates a room, a seeker expresses interest, and the host's dashboard renders the enquiry row under the Flatmate tab. Clicking Accept persists the decision to the server (verified via API read-back). The retired mock twin (`owner-id-inbox`) read a `localStorage`-keyed bucket that could split by mobile; the live spec proves the `ownerId` bucket works end-to-end against the real API (D186) | consumer/account | consumer/flatmates/host-inbox | ✅ |
| A room with no seat open refuses a new ask: `POST /flatmates/rooms/{id}/interest` answers 409 `room_full` ("This room is full right now.") for a seat room at 0 or a split room with no space for the asked people; nothing is recorded and the seeker's Send interest button stays usable. Asks sent before it filled still reach the host (FM-24) | flatmates | consumer/flatmates/host-inbox ("a room with no seats left refuses a new ask…", web 1280 + phone 360), backend `FlatmateAcceptanceEndpointsTest.fullRoomRefusesNewAsks` | ✅ |
| **Owner-consent OTP flow against the live API** — the two-step `POST /flatmates/owner-consent` endpoint (title and locality name the flat): `null` otp sends the code (`consentRecorded: false`), `"000000"` records consent (`consentRecorded: true`), and the group card renders the "Owner-consented" chip in the browser. Self-consent (own number) is rejected by the server. Second test: the consent button stays disabled until a full 10-digit mobile is entered — a browser-only validation guard | consumer/flatmates | consumer/flatmates/owner-consent | ✅ |
| **Owner consent taken before the group exists** — `OwnerConsentModal` now drives `POST /flatmates/owner-consent` through the seam (send, then record) instead of writing `setOwnerConsent()` to localStorage, so the OTP round-trip the tenant does with their landlord finally reaches the server. Proven end-to-end: after completing the modal in the browser, a group created with that `consentMobile` comes back `ownerConsent: true` — a flag `FlatmateMapper` refuses to accept from a client, so only a row the server wrote can produce it | consumer/flatmates | consumer/flatmates/owner-consent | ✅ |
| **Tenant badge consent and registered-agreement evidence** — posting remains available without owner consent, but Ops approval is refused until the owner completes its OTP. The agreement step asks only for the document (no registration number or dates — the uploaded agreement carries them, and Ops reads them off it); the flag plus the document queue the post for Ops. The form also states the tenant disclosure and links the no-agreement path to Leave & License service registration. | consumer/flatmates, ops | consumer/flatmates/tenant-badge-consent-and-registration | ✅ |
| **A consent names one flat** — `POST /flatmates/owner-consent`, the route the group form actually calls, now carries the title and locality the host typed, and V30 keys the stored row on that address. Proven by the pair: a group with the consented title comes back `ownerConsent: true`, while a second group by the same host naming the same owner's number comes back `false` and its Ops approval is refused 422. Without the scope one OTP would vouch for every sub-let that host ever posts, including flats the owner has never heard of | consumer/flatmates, ops | consumer/flatmates/tenant-badge-consent-and-registration | ✅ |
| **Entering the verify funnel grants no badge** — both profile entry points ("Verify identity" and the "ID not verified" header chip) navigate to `/verify-identity`, and `/auth/me` plus a fresh page load still report the account unverified. The paired test grants the badge through the real staff-decision path and proves both CTAs retire together. A client that could talk itself into a trust badge is a security defect, and this is what would notice | trust-and-verification | platform/auth/verify-funnel | ✅ |
| The **rendered** "ID verified" pill on `/dashboard?tab=profile` once the badge is granted, and both funnel CTAs retiring together (D21). The grant is driven through `POST /me/verification/aadhaar/simulate` — the `@LocalOnly` endpoint built for this in D122 — which runs the production `handleWebhook` path, so this stands on a real grant rather than a faked one. Paired with the row above on purpose: "starting grants nothing" would pass just as happily against a badge feature that never worked at all | trust-and-verification | platform/auth/verify-funnel | ✅ |
| `/owner-hub` + `/owner-hub/property/:id` passport | consumer/account | managed-properties, consumer/property/property-passport | ✅ |
| **The owner hub through the `managed` seam** — Rent-o-meter registration, the passport read, the document vault, rent tracking and publish all route through `managedService` / `documentService` rather than `lib/data/managedProperty.js` and `draazyDocs:`, so the same specs stand against both providers. Every one of these paths is now asynchronous and every write can be refused, which is the point: publish re-runs listing validation server-side and answers 422 on a record captured loosely enough to be legal as a private draft and illegal as an advertisement (D32) | dashboard-owner-hub | consumer/account/dashboard, consumer/property/property-passport | ✅ |
| **A rent receipt is a server-held immutable snapshot with a durable id** (D248) — the tracker used to keep its ledger in `localStorage` and mint the reference as `'RCPT' + Date.now()`, so a month marked received on a phone was unpaid on the laptop, the number on the tenant's copy changed on every re-download, and the figures were re-read from the *current* property record, which meant raising the rent quietly rewrote last year's receipts. `POST /me/managed-properties/{id}/rent-receipts` now derives amount, tenant, landlord and address server-side from the owned property and stores them verbatim; the browser sends only `rentMonth`. The load-bearing assertions are the ones a mock cannot reach: the POST on the wire, a re-read **outside the browser**, and a **second browser context** seeing the same settled month. Duplicate creation is deterministic — the UI replaces the button with the download link, and the server answers the retry `409` while the original id is unchanged, because a re-issue would leave the tenant holding a reference nothing matches. The 422s (not rented, no rent, no tenant) and the foreign/unknown-id `404` stay in `ManagedRentReceiptTest`, where the transaction rolls back instead of leaving wreckage in the shared database. Pay Rent is untouched: those are gateway payments whose paid state is webhook-controlled | dashboard-owner-hub | rent-receipts | ✅ |
| `/view-documents/:requestId` secure viewer | consumer/account | consumer/account/view-documents-flow, consumer/account/doc-viewer-scheme | ✅ |
| **A granted buyer opens their own documents without the owner forwarding anything** — the buyer's half of the document gate against the live API. The submit was already covered (`platform/verification-disclaimer`); the **read** was not, and that is where the promise lived. `shareToken` is owner-facing by contract — it is minted so the owner can forward the link to a lawyer deliberately — so before `GET /me/document-requests/{reqId}/documents` a buyer whose owner never forwarded it saw the status "Granted" and a dead end. The load-bearing assertions are the negative ones: neither the requester projection nor the owner's inbox row carries a `shareToken` key at all (it is minted and stored server-side only), and the buyer's per-file `GET .../documents/{docId}/url` signs a file only against that buyer's live grant; a *pending* request reports `sharedDocumentCount: 0` and its document read is a 404, because an ask is not permission to inventory a stranger's vault; and a stranger's 404 is compared **body-for-body** (minus `traceId`) with the 404 for an id that never existed, since two distinguishable refusals restore the existence oracle the shared status code was chosen to remove. No mock can hold an opinion about any of this — there is no token in it to redact and no reason to hide anything from itself. Also pins **register 37**: the `document.granted` notification's `link` is asserted to equal `/view-documents/{reqId}` and the viewer is then opened **by that link** rather than by a URL the spec assembles, so a server that stopped emitting the deep link cannot pass; and the stranger's browser sees the neutral "Access not available" — the same words for pending, lapsed, unknown and foreign, because a screen that distinguishes them undoes the shared 404. Builds and removes its own `Society NOC` file (deliberately not the `Sale Deed` slot `property-integration` occupies) and uses throwaway buyers, never `ACTORS.buyer`, whose `p5021` request the disclaimer spec expects to find pending | dashboard-owner-hub, documents | consumer/account/buyer-document-access | ✅ |
| **Offers have a floor, accepted offers are final, and finalizing uses the agreed price** — an offer or counter under half the asking price is refused 422 with the minimum named, the offer input keeps digits only in Indian grouping (`25,00,000` + "≈ ₹ 25 Lakh") and names the floor before sending, an accepted offer shows no Revise button and a counter on it is a plain 409, the finalize request's `agreedPrice` is the accepted offer (not the listing price), `GET /finalization/{id}/status` answers 204 (no console error) when nothing was requested, and an owner's finalize-accept on a rent listing opens a pending tenancy with a repeat accept answering 409 rather than 500 | deals-offers-finalization | consumer/account/deals-offers ("an offer field keeps digits only", "after the owner accepts") | ✅ |
| **Finalize rental modal validates each field before sending** — name, mobile (digits only, max 10) and price (Indian grouping) each show a field message that clears once fixed, the date cannot be in the future and defaults to today's local date, and a double-click on Confirm & Finalize closes the deal once | deals-offers-finalization | consumer/account/deals-offers ("Finalize rental validates each field") | ✅ |
| **Add transaction explains a missing category or amount, and a double-click on Save records one row** | owner-finances | consumer/account/owner-finances ("Add transaction explains what is missing") | ✅ |
| **Owner Finances reads top-down on a phone**: the header holds only the property and period pickers; one summary card leads with net cashflow, health and trend, then collected and expenses; Activity filters with All / Income / Expense pills beside one Add button (no floating FAB); the CSV/PDF statement lives in Reports and exports the server rows, not the local ledger. Untested: touch feel on real hardware | owner-finances | consumer/account/owner-finances ("owner finance transaction drives the API summary…": net figure, Expense pill, Reports CSV download) | ✅ |
| **Deals / Offers / Negotiation / Finalization** — incl. a declined finalize request surfacing "the owner hasn't confirmed — ask again" now the status read returns terminal rows, not pending only (D111) | deals-offers-finalization | consumer/account/deals-offers | ✅ |
| **Rent-deal tenancy confirmation** — closing a rent deal creates a *pending* tenancy, the named tenant sees it on `/dashboard#rental` with no rent tools, confirms it (it becomes active) or declines it (deal stays closed, no tenancy), and anyone else gets 404 on both actions | rent-tenancy | consumer/account/tenancy-confirmation | ✅ |
| **Buyer deal visibility on the property page** — a buyer on a *closed* listing sees the terminal "no longer available / rented out" banner and the offer + finalize cards are hidden; a *reserved* listing shows the "Under Offer" banner but keeps the offer UI (still queueable); an untouched listing shows no banner and the normal negotiate card. Reads the property's public `dealStatus` mirror, not the owner-scoped deal (D110) | deals-offers-finalization | consumer/property/deal-visibility | ✅ |
| English-only UI: `<html lang="en">`, no raw keys or `[object Object]`, a stale `dzLang`/`i18nextLng` from an older build is ignored, Settings has no language choice | — | platform/i18n (chromium + mobile), platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| Dashboard ▸ Profile & Settings **behind a real session** — reduce motion and the delete-account confirmation gate. Reduce motion genuinely is a device setting with no provider in front of it (a root class), so what the conversion changed for them is that the *person* is real: a preference keyed by mobile only means anything if the mobile belongs to an account the server agrees exists. The owner card is the sharper case — `isOwner` is derived from real inventory, so the seeded version had to hand itself a fabricated listing to make the card appear at all, where Meera's four make it render for the same reason it renders in production | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| **Full name accepts letters only** — any script (Devanagari included), spaces and `. ' -`, starting with a letter, 2–80 chars. Profile, signup, the sign-in name step and the inline OTP sheet share `isValidName`; the server's `UserUpdate` `@Pattern` is the same rule (`MeEndpointsTest`). The Profile save of `6516%^&$*@#^` shows the error and sends no `PATCH` | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| Settings ▸ Appearance ▸ **Light mode** — a device setting (`dzAppPrefs.theme`), **on by default**: `html.light`, light page background, `theme-color` meta and favicon (light tile, teal-600 mark), brand teal fills (`bg-teal-500` + white text) untouched; switching it off restores dark, and the dark choice survives a reload (pre-paint script in `index.html`) | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| Admin / ops header ▸ **Light mode** toggle — the Sun/Moon button in `AdminLayout` flips `html.light` for both staff portals, survives a reload, and `.dz-card` tiles paint a shade off the light page (never pure white) | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| Consumer account menu ▸ **theme toggle** (`theme-toggle`) — a small moon/sun icon beside the name in the desktop dropdown and beside the close button in the phone drawer; flips `html.light` and survives a reload | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| **The owner privacy switches are account state, not device state (DM).** They sat in `dzOwnerPrefs:<mobile>` — a key that survived nothing and travelled nowhere, so a preference about who may reach you was answered by whichever browser happened to ask. Both now ride `PATCH /auth/me` (`hideNumber`, `verifiedContactOnly`) and come back on every `/auth/me`, `/auth/login` and `/auth/staff-login`. The two are asserted differently on purpose: `hideNumber` is checked at the UI as a *request* (`postDataJSON().hideNumber === true`) because the old spec asserted a localStorage key that live mode no longer writes — an assertion that would have gone on passing while reading a value nothing produced; `verifiedContactOnly` is checked at the API on a throwaway account, because it is the one with teeth (`ContactService.request` throws `VerificationRequiredException`) and flipping it on a fixture actor would change the answer every other spec gets when asking to reach her. The `hideNumber` press is undone in the same test for the same reason. Copy is load-bearing here too: `hideNumber` is inert today (zero `isHideNumber` reads in `src/main`), so the card describes it as a recorded preference rather than a guarantee | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| **Download my data is answered by the server (DN/DL).** It used to be `exportUserData()` reading the browser's own localStorage — a subject-access export that could only ever return what this device happened to hold. `GET /me/data-export` returns 73 datasets over 9 domains with the redaction rule stated in the document itself, and is rate-limited through `WriteRateLimitFilter.LIMITED_READS`. The load-bearing assertion is the negative one: a counterparty's display name (`Meera Deshpande`) must not appear anywhere in `datasets`, because the redaction is stricter than briefed — counterparties surface only as an opaque salted `partyRef`, and an export that leaks the other side of a deal is a privacy incident dressed as a feature. Server-side twin: `DataExportCoverageTest` | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| **Notification preferences are a server document, not a browser one** — the row above used to include them and said they "stay in localStorage after the mock retires". That was wrong about the platform even when written: `GET`/`PUT /me/notification-preferences` had existed since D94/D15 with tests and an OpenAPI entry, and nothing in `frontend/src` had ever called it, so the server enforced none of these settings — a quiet-hours window suppressed the alerts the *client* derived and nothing else, and a notification the server wrote at 03:00 arrived at 03:00. Two tests: a channel toggle is asserted as a `PUT` carrying **all six** required fields (a partial body 422s, and a swallowed 422 looks exactly like a success); and quiet hours + match alerts set in one browser are read back in a **second browser context** with its own storage and session, both flipped away from their defaults so a fallback-to-defaults regression cannot pass | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| Notification Preferences ▸ Delivery channels offer **no SMS switch** — Draazy sends no SMS notifications (OTP-by-SMS is login, not a preference). The `sms` field stays in the `PUT` body because the server contract requires all six | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |
| **Redirects** (`/map`, `/docs`, `/help-center`, `/share-flat`, `/owner-hub`) + `/admin/support` serving the Support queue rather than forwarding + the 404 catch-all | — | platform/route-redirects-404 | ✅ |
| **A customer asks for a refund from Plan & Billing, and the entitlement freezes until staff decide.** `GET /me/payments` replaces the mock history: each row carries its kind, invoice number, status and a `refund` verdict. An unused Contact Pack shows "Refund · until <date>"; asking (Idempotency-Key, replay returns the same row) puts it in Refund pending and freezes its contacts (30 → 15); the desk approves and the row ends Refunded with its credit note. A rejection restores the pack (back to 30), shows "Refund declined" with an "Ask for a review" link, and the same order answers `already_requested`. Reasons read off the row: `not_eligible_used`, `window_closed`; for a Listing Pass `window_not_open` (with the opening date), `pass_no_enquiries`, an enquiry flips it to `not_eligible_used`, `window_closed`, `listing_paused` and `guarantee_used_recently`. Asking freezes the pass and pauses its listing; a rejection resumes it. Another user's order is 404, signed out 401. **The signed gateway webhook is machine-local, so the invoice and credit-note rows are inserted where it would write them** (`helpers/refunds.js seedInvoice/seedCreditNote`); the 5-minute gateway-refund sweep is not run | plans-billing-refer | consumer/services/refund-request | ✅ |
| **Contacts come back.** An owner declining returns the contact (remaining 14 → 15), shows the "Contact returned" chip, sends a `contact.returned` notification, and a re-ask stays declined. A request unanswered past `contactReplyDays` stops counting (backdated `created_at`) and the owner's accept is 409; stored expiry (the daily sweep, emulated by SQL) lets the seeker ask again at the cost of a new contact. `contactPendingMax` (set to 1 through the settings API, restored) refuses the next press with 422 `contact_pending_limit` and the toast, and an answer frees the slot. A paused listing refuses a new request 409 `listing_not_live` ("isn't taking enquiries right now") and the owner's Accept says "Make the listing live to share your number." until it is resumed. A lapsed request reads `expired` from `GET /contacts/status`, so the property page shows the Contact returned chip beside a still-live Request number button | contact-gate-leads | consumer/property/contact-returns | ✅ |
| **Refund Policy and Terms read as the new policy.** Eleven sections with the windows read from `/bootstrap` (reply days, pack window, guarantee days and the once-in-N-months limit), a refund button on Plan & Billing, a credit note, no 25%/50% tiers and no suspension clause, the Grievance Officer named from settings with a mailto link, "48 hours" acknowledgement and "one month" resolution; the contents list reaches the officer section; Terms say a pass ends with no refund for unused days and link to the policy | legal | platform/refund-policy | ✅ |

## Mobile chrome & ergonomics

Mobile-only behaviour has no desktop equivalent to piggy-back on, so it gets its own
table rather than a column in the route matrix above.

| Behaviour | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Bottom tab bar: mount, destinations, raised Post slot (a dialog opener, not a link — it opens the app-wide posting sheet) | mobile/bottom-nav | ✅ |
| Bottom inset reservation (`.has-bottom-nav` / `--dz-bottom-inset`) | mobile/bottom-inset | ✅ |
| Safe-area insets, `theme-color`, manifest link | mobile/safe-area, mobile/pwa | ✅ |
| **The `/listings` platform layer — five declarations that decide whether the page reads as an installed app or as a website in a browser.** None is visible in a screenshot and none is reachable from the desktop project, so all five rotted silently until this spec. `interactive-widget=resizes-content` on the viewport meta: without it Android Chrome overlays the keyboard rather than shrinking the viewport, and the filter drawer's "Show N results" — a fixed bottom bar — is unreachable behind it the moment a budget field takes focus. `overscroll-behavior-y: contain` on the **root**, not on body: `usePullToRefresh` skips its `preventDefault` once the gesture is no longer cancellable, so a fast flick armed both the app's indicator and Chrome's own, and body's value can never propagate to the viewport because `html` carries `overflow-x: clip`. The same containment on `.filter-panel` *and* `.filter-scroll` — the panel is the fixed box, the inner element is the one that actually overflows, and containing only one still chains the results page behind a modal drawer. `scroll-behavior: auto` under `prefers-reduced-motion` **and** under the app's own `html.dz-reduce-motion` toggle, since paging a long result list is otherwise a long animated jump for someone who asked for none — asserted on both halves, and the test stubs `window.scrollTo` to assert `goToPage` passes **no** `behavior` at all: the default `auto` defers to the computed `scroll-behavior`, whereas naming one would outrank the stylesheet and reinstate the animation. And the drawer's action bar clearing the home indicator. The last is asserted against the **declared** value through a CSSOM walk that matches rules against the element rather than transcribing the Tailwind arbitrary class into the test, where it would drift the moment the utility changed: emulation reports every `env(safe-area-inset-*)` as 0, so the computed padding is 12px on a notched iPhone and on a headless Pixel alike and distinguishes nothing. That walk matches *before* recursing — CSS Nesting gives every plain `CSSStyleRule` an empty `cssRules`, and an `else`/`continue` branch swallows the entire sheet (it did, and reported the declaration missing) | mobile/listings-platform-layer | ✅ |
| **The map detail sheet's own three fixes are unasserted** — `86dvh` instead of `86vh` (the URL bar makes `vh` taller than the screen, cutting off the footer action), `touch-action: pinch-zoom` on `.dz-mdp` with `pan-y pinch-zoom` handed back to `.dz-mdp-scroll` (`pinch-zoom` rather than `none` on both, or the sheet — the whole listing on a phone — stops being magnifiable; plus `data-no-ptr` on the sheet, without which a downward grabber drag also arms `usePullToRefresh`, since `touch-action` governs browser panning and does nothing to a JS `touchmove` listener), and the grabber actually dismissing on a downward drag. Reaching `.dz-mdp` at phone width needs the Maps SDK and live pins, and the two properties above have no signature under emulation in any case; verified on hardware and tracked in `tasks/todo.md` | mobile/listings-platform-layer (documented gap) | 🟡 |
| **The `/reels` platform layer — the tells that give a full-screen swipe feed away as a website.** `overscroll-behavior-y: contain` on `.reel-wrap`: the feed is an *inner* scroller, and `overscroll-behavior` does not inherit, so the root's own containment never covered it — a flick past the last reel chained out to the document and rubber-banded the page background into view under a surface meant to be edge-to-edge media. The intent filter's `:hover` was ungated, so on touch the first tap left a chip lit until the next tap landed elsewhere and an unselected filter read as selected; it now sits under `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` with an `:active` scale taking over as the press response, asserted as a declaration (matched on the scale factor, since `transform: none` would satisfy a test for the word) because `:active` cannot be forced headless. Chips measured ~32px against the 44px floor and gained a `min-height`. The rail's short-viewport override is the find the spec is really guarding: `@media (max-height: 700px)` asked for 42px icons, but it was declared *before* the base `.rail .ic` rule and a media query contributes no specificity, so it lost on source order and had never once applied — the same silent-no-op shape as the `else`/`continue` bug in the `/listings` walk below. Moved after the base rule and raised to 44px, so the block now does what its comment always claimed. And `env(safe-area-inset-left/right)` on `.rail`, `.reel-info` and `.reels-top`: `viewport-fit=cover` already puts the page under the sensor housing, so in landscape the action rail sat beneath it. The insets are asserted against the **declared** value through a CSSOM walk keyed on selector text rather than on a matched element — `:hover` and `:active` rules match nothing at rest, and their absence is the thing being looked for. Emulation reports every inset as 0, so the landscape half is confirmed on a device | mobile/reels-platform-layer | ✅ |
| PWA manifest + icon set | mobile/pwa | ✅ |
| Home-screen install nudge: engagement gate, escalating silence, platform split | mobile/install-prompt | ✅ |
| Chrome policy per route (`lib/chrome.js`) — footer/bottom-nav/assistant | mobile/bottom-nav, mobile/inbox-saved, platform/feature-flags | ✅ |
| **Every Application flag is honoured end to end against the server** — the 11 kept switches each asserted off and back on: `mapSearch`, `scheduleVisit`, `reviewsEnabled`, `inAppMessaging`, `assistant`, `kycBadgeEnabled` in the page and at their route guards; `subscriptionPlans` as a paused checkout **and** a 403 `purchases_paused` from `POST /me/subscription`; `signupsEnabled` as a 403 `signups_closed` for a new number while an existing account still signs in; `staffLoginEnabled` refusing a staff password but never an admin; `maintenanceMode` as the consumer overlay (with no link to staff sign-in, which stays open by direct URL) plus a 503 `maintenance_mode` on writes. A flag missing from the document reads as on (maintenance as off) in both the client and the console. Retired flags (`compareProperties`, `savedListings`, `emiCalculator`, `videoListings`, `paidFeaturedListings`, …) stored as off hide nothing. The slug-less `/society` route and the `societySaaS` flag are gone: the path is the not-found page and `GET /bootstrap` carries no such flag. The flag is written through `PUT /admin/settings` and read through `GET /bootstrap`, so nothing in the test touches what the page reads | platform/feature-flags | ✅ |
| **The product survives losing every feature** — the listings and property pages still render with every kept consumer flag off and zero `pageerror`s. The seeded ancestors of these two tests read `draazyDB_v1`, a store key three versions stale; `JSON.parse(null)` returned null, the guard returned silently, and both spent their lives asserting that a page with **all flags enabled** renders cleanly, under a name claiming the opposite (`maintenanceMode` is excluded on purpose — switching it on blanks the app, so an all-off smoke test would assert the maintenance screen rather than the product) | platform/feature-flags | ✅ |
| **No mobile chrome leaks onto desktop** | platform/desktop-noleak-guardrails | ✅ |
| **The application actually boots** — four routes (`/`, `/listings`, `/dashboard` as owner, `/admin` as admin) render a non-empty body with zero `pageerror`s. This exists because `npm run check`, `npm run lint` and `npm run check:size` were all green on 2026-08-11 while the app served an empty `<body>` on every route: a Vite build resolves an import cycle happily and only a browser executes it, so no static gate can see the failure (D208) | platform/boot-canary | ✅ |
| 44px tap-target sweep across consumer routes | mobile/tap-targets | ✅ |
| 44px sweep behind a session (`/dashboard`, `/messages`) and on staff surfaces (`/admin`, `/staff`) | mobile/tap-targets | ✅ |
| The sweep cannot pass vacuously: it waits on `appReady()` rather than `networkidle`, refuses to report a pass on fewer than `MIN_CANDIDATES` interactive elements, follows the client-side navigation with `waitForURL` before measuring the property page, skips `pointer-events: none` subtrees (closed off-canvas drawers) and measures `::before`/`::after` hit areas rather than trusting the `.tap-extend` class name | mobile/tap-targets | ✅ |
| The sweep cannot fail spuriously either: after the candidate floor is met it re-measures until the undersized list is empty (5s cap) before asserting. Controls drawn at exactly `w-11 h-11` measure 44.000px at rest — zero margin against the floor — so a single measurement taken while card images were still landing recorded 43.99 and failed. A genuinely undersized control never clears, so the poll times out and the assertion still fails with the full evidence. Failure sizes are reported to two decimals, not rounded, so a sub-pixel shortfall cannot print as "44" | mobile/tap-targets | ✅ |
| 12px computed-font-size floor across 14 routes, signed-out, signed-in and admin | mobile/text-legibility | ✅ |

| **Smart-search submit is a circle floating inside the pill** (`/listings`, `/flatmates`) — its diameter is asserted to be the bar's height minus the 4px inset on each side, so the two can never drift apart. Only the flatmates one derives that from the bar; the listings twin sits on a flex track shared with the bell and is a literal `w-9 h-9`, so this spec is the only thing holding the pair in step. The first flatmates build hard-coded a 44px circle inside a 40px bar and broke both edges of the thing containing it. The 36px circle is under the touch floor, so the spec also reads the real `::before` geometry and hit-tests the extended corners with `elementsFromPoint` — a transparent extension is invisible to the eye and to `boundingBox()`, and a `pointer-events: none` pseudo would satisfy the generic 44px sweep while catching nothing. Red-checked by deleting `tap-extend` | mobile/search-submit-shape | ✅ |

| **`/flatmates` is not sticky-hover, not short-thumbed and not under the home indicator** — three route-local regressions the generic sweeps miss by construction. The card lift and the ghost-button hover were ungated, so the synthetic hover a tap leaves behind held a tapped card raised until the user tapped elsewhere; asserted as a vertical offset rather than `transform: none`, since `.reveal` settles on an identity matrix and animates in (poll, don't read). `.btn-ghost` is the one class with no rule in `components/buttons.css` at all, so it alone skipped the height every other button class carries and the live-request row's Edit / Mark filled / **Delete** came out 32px; the floor tracks `--btn-h` behind `pointer: coarse` so the deliberately compact `h-8`/`h-9` pills keep their size on a mouse, and the spec pairs a rendered-geometry sweep with a count floor (most ghosts need a signed-in author or a joined group, so a thin render would otherwise report a pass on a sample of one) and a check that no ghost escapes the `.sf-page` scope. And the drawer is `position: fixed` against three edges, so at `viewport-fit=cover` its Reset / Show-results row — the last of the scroll — lands in the home-indicator zone; all four sides moved out of the element's Tailwind `p-6` into the route rule, since half-here-half-there drifts silently. Emulation reports every `env(safe-area-inset-*)` as 0, so the bottom inset is driven through `--dz-safe-b` and the top/left are read off the declaration via CSSOM. The `/listings` twin pads its own pinned action row, which is why this is scoped and not added to the shared `.filter-panel`. Red-checked: all three fail with the route stylesheet stashed | mobile/flatmates-touch-targets | ✅ |

| **Every scroll lock in the app was a no-op** — `index.css` gives html `overflow-x: clip`, and body's overflow only propagates to the viewport while the root's own is `visible`, so the `document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden'` all 17 overlays carried (`Modal`, `Select`/`MultiSelect` sheets, the navbar drawer, the help drawer, the property modals, the lightbox, `ReportModal`, …) set a property nothing reads. `/flatmates` found it because it was the only route with no lock at all: `.sf-modal` is used by nothing outside it, so neither the app-wide sweep nor the route sweep above owned it. All 17 now share `hooks/useScrollLock.js`, which writes the root and reference-counts, because overlays nest — a `Select` inside a `Modal`, the owner-consent sheet over the flat-share form — and without the count the inner one's cleanup releases the outer one's lock. Asserted on the root's computed overflow rather than by gesture: the backdrop covers the viewport and already contains its own overscroll, so the page stays put either way, and `window.scrollTo` succeeds straight through `overflow: hidden` — neither probe can tell a live lock from a dead one. Paired with the same read after Escape, which is the half a mis-counted release breaks. The element is also the scroller, hence `overscroll-behavior: contain`, and its submit row is the last of that scroll, so at `viewport-fit=cover` it was landing in the home-indicator zone: the bottom inset is driven through `--dz-safe-b` and measured as a delta, since the base gutter is viewport-relative. `dvh` is read off the declaration because emulation resolves it identically to `vh`. Both intents are driven through `?post=`, which needs a signed-in account — the reason this is a separate signed-in spec and not a case in the one above. Red-checked: reverting one sheet to the body idiom reports `visible` | mobile/flatmates-modals | ✅ |

| **The detail page's tab strip scrolled on both axes, and its header opened with the wrong facts.** `overflow-x: auto` forces `overflow-y: auto` on the same box, and `.dz-detail-tab`'s `margin-bottom: -1px` handed that axis a few pixels of range — enough that a thumb aiming to scroll the page instead wobbled the strip. The lock is `overflow-y: hidden` on `.hscroll-row:not([data-axis-free])` under `@media (pointer: coarse) and (max-width: 639.98px)`: coarse so the active tab's underline cannot clip on a desktop hairline, the width so the rails carrying an `sm:`/`md:overflow-visible` escape hatch are not clipped on a tablet, the opt-out for `Tabs.jsx`, which stops being a scroll container below `sm` on purpose. `touch-action: pan-x` was rejected because it makes the browser discard vertical gestures that start on the rail rather than passing them to the page, which the wheel assertion here is what pins. Asserted as `overflowY === 'hidden'` and NOT as `scrollHeight - clientHeight`: an `overflow: hidden` box is still a scroll container and still reports its overflow region, so that difference stays non-zero either way. The second half is the phone reading order: views, shortlists and quality score are social proof, not facts a buyer decides on, and they are ordered below the trust panel by CSS `order` alone — the DOM keeps its desktop sequence, which is why the property-page specs' selectors are untouched. Asserted against that neighbour rather than against the viewport, so a seed with a taller hero cannot fail it as if the ordering had regressed. The stat block is asserted to be a flat ledger (no per-row surface, label and value on one line) rather than a grid of bordered tiles, and a buy listing (`p5013`) is visited separately because a buy is the deal type that renders the ₹/sq.ft tile at all | property-detail | mobile/property-density | ✅ |

"Rent / sq.ft" was removed for rentals only: a tenant compares a monthly figure against other rentals and never a rate per foot, while a buyer genuinely trades ₹/sq.ft off against locality benchmarks. Both rules are one-sided, so the test visits a buy and a rental and asserts the presence half as well as the absence — an assertion that a string is gone passes trivially on a page that renders neither | property-detail | mobile/property-density ("the price block carries only figures the buyer acts on") | ✅ |

Both halves are asserted, in the project that can see each — the phone spec pins the navbar tile visible, the desktop spec pins the breadcrumb's crumb resolving to a listings `href` — *before* counting the pill's absence, since a page that never rendered satisfies the absence on its own. The pill's behaviour survives split across the two: it did `navigate(-1)` when history existed and `navigate(returnTo, {state:{restore:true}})` otherwise, which is exactly the navbar tile and the breadcrumb respectively. `property.backToResults`/`backToMap` deleted from all three locales | property-detail | mobile/property-density ("up-navigation is offered once, by the navbar tile"), consumer/property/detail-improvements ("up-navigation is the breadcrumb, and nothing repeats it") | ✅ |
| **Decorative hover states cannot be left stuck on a touchscreen, and the lightbox owns its gesture.** A touchscreen synthesises `:hover` on whatever a finger last landed on and holds it until the next tap lands elsewhere, so an ungated decorative `:hover` is a state the user has no way to clear — a tile left glowing as if selected, a hero left 5% zoomed by the tap that opened the lightbox, a visit-type `.pick` left wearing a paler copy of the teal that `.pick.sel` paints. Fifteen selectors on this page are gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`, which keeps the trackpad iPad in and rules out the Android devices that advertise a hover they cannot deliver; four more card lifts that ship in the same global sheet — the owner's property rows and the service landing tiles — are asserted from here too, since the CSSOM carries them without loading their routes (`.svc-card` on the services hub is gated but unasserted, as it ships in that route's own chunk). Asserted two ways, because either alone is weak: a CSSOM walk proves each selector still *exists* and is wrapped (a renamed rule would otherwise pass as "gated"), and a real `.hover()` on `.detail-card` proves the computed paint does not move — the second would still catch a future hover written as an inline style or a class toggle. The walk guards itself with `seen > 100` and matches before recursing, since CSS Nesting gives every plain style rule a normally-empty `cssRules` that an `else if` branch would swallow the whole sheet into; the touch test first asserts the emulation really reports the gate as false, or it would pass vacuously. The lightbox half: all three overlays using `.dz-lightbox` are hand-rolled divs rather than `<Modal>`, so none inherits its body scroll lock, and `touch-action: pinch-zoom` on the overlay stops the photo's 40px swipe from also panning the listing behind it and a downward swipe from firing pull-to-refresh, while keeping pinch for the floor plan, which exists to be enlarged to read room dimensions; it sits on the overlay because touch-action intersects down the tree, so `none` there would cancel any pinch on the image. The arrows are hidden on coarse pointers. Safe-area insets and `dvh` are asserted against the *declared* value via the CSSOM, not the computed one: Chromium emulation reports `env(safe-area-inset-*)` as 0 and resolves `dvh` exactly like `vh`, so they have no behavioural signature and are verified on hardware | property-detail | mobile/touch-states | ✅ |
| **The hero is a real scroll container, so the finger drives the photo instead of a threshold measuring it afterwards.** The gallery was one `<img>` whose `src` a `touchend` handler swapped once a drag exceeded 40px — no rubber-banding, no partial travel, nothing under the finger until it lifted. It is now a `snap-x snap-mandatory` track of slides, which also means the page no longer has to be told which axis the element owns. Four seams are pinned because each breaks silently: the container really is `overflow-x: auto` with mandatory snapping and `overscroll-x: contain` (a swipe past the last photo must not scroll the listing), plus `overflow-y: hidden` — `overflow-x: auto` alone computes y to `auto`, so an overhang let the photo drag vertically under a horizontal swipe; the flatmate room page reuses this track and `consumer/flatmates/detail-page` asserts the same y-axis lock there; a settled scroll moves the dot rail *and* the counter pill, asserted with auto-retrying matchers because the commit is on a 120ms settle timer rather than per frame — committing mid-drag re-enters the sync effect and yanks the track out from under a finger that has merely paused, which is why a `dragging` ref distinguishes "finger stopped moving" from "finger released"; the reverse direction, a dot tap dragging the track, which is the half that dies quietly when the dots, thumbnails, desktop arrows or lightbox move `active` without the scroller following; and the request-photos card being the genuine last child with nothing past it. Above `sm` the same track goes `overflow: hidden` and the arrows drive it programmatically, so the desktop frame is unchanged. The dots are a labelled group of buttons carrying `aria-current`, not a tablist: `role="tab"` promises an `aria-controls` panel shown while its siblings are hidden, and every slide here is in the DOM and swipeable at all times. One dot per photo, capped at the 10 both the uploader and `ListingCreate`/`ListingUpdate` accept, so a dot always means the photo its label names; the assertions pin the second photo to the second dot and the last photo to the last photo dot, plus the invariant that exactly one dot is ever lit. That last case is the defect the rail shipped with: a dot index was compared straight against `active` under a six-dot cap, so on a fuller gallery every photo past the sixth left the whole rail dark. What no emulator shows: momentum, rubber-banding and whether the photo tracks the finger 1:1 | property-detail | mobile/touch-states | ✅ |
| **Content budget: primary content is reachable, not buried under chrome** | mobile/content-budget | ✅ |
| Assistant coach-mark: never covers the price, budgeted to 2 sightings | mobile/content-budget | ✅ |
| Top-bar scroll behaviour | mobile/topbar-scroll | ✅ |
| Dashboard tabs dock flush at the top edge when the top bar hides on scroll (`dz-docks-under-nav`), leaving no see-through gap | mobile/dashboard-nav ("the dashboard tabs rise to the top edge...") | ✅ |
| Context-aware navbar left slot / account pill | mobile/navbar-context | ✅ |
| Signed-in phone top bar holds 6 visible targets at 360px, not 7 — Compare moves to the account drawer below `lg` and `/compare` stays reachable there (D98b) | mobile/navbar-context ("sheds Compare to the account drawer") | ✅ |
| On-screen keyboard handling in auth | mobile/auth-keyboard | ✅ |
| Bottom nav hides while a text field has the soft keyboard (cookie bar, install prompt and assistant FAB share the same `data-kbd-hide` hook); a shorter viewport with no field focused is not a keyboard | mobile/keyboard-chrome | ✅ |
| Landscape orientation | mobile/landscape | ✅ |
| Space/density optimisation | mobile/space-optimization | ✅ |
| Ops console on a field phone | mobile/ops-field | ✅ |
| Dashboard hub navigation on a phone | mobile/dashboard-hub | ✅ |
| Phase-3 sweep | mobile/phase3 | ✅ |
| Touch fakes `:hover` on first tap and holds it, so every card lift is gated on `@media (hover: hover)` — the same query Tailwind's `hoverOnlyWhenSupported` compiles `hover:` utilities behind, so the two never disagree on a hover-capable coarse pointer. Covers the `index.css` rules and the two scoped halves that outrank them, `routes/saved.css` and `routes/listings.css`. The witness is `.cat-card`, not the home Featured card, whose `list-reveal` animation pins its transform and would pass either way | mobile/phase3 ("a tapped card is not left hovering"), platform/desktop-noleak-guardrails ("the card lift survives the hover gate") | ✅ |
| Platform baseline: `touch-action: manipulation` (no double-tap-zoom click delay), `-webkit-text-size-adjust: 100%` (no landscape inflation), `color-scheme: dark` (no light UA chrome on a dark page), `user-select: none` on controls but re-enabled on fields, since `label` wraps its input in ~90 places and inherited `none` costs iOS its long-press Paste | mobile/phase3 ("the platform baseline is declared") | ✅ |
| **Date/time pickers are floating cards below 640px** — every date, time and month field (incl. the dashboard HRA receipt months, now a month `Select`); no native `type="date|time|month"` input is left to open the OS picker (`check-ui-std.mjs` `nativePicker`) | mobile/date-time-fields | ✅ |
| **Searchable dropdowns open as a full-height picker below 640px**, search first and the list ending 12px above the keyboard (or the bottom nav) (`useVisualViewportInsets` → `--dz-vv-top/bottom`), because iOS lays the keyboard over a bottom-docked sheet. The scrim shrinks to a strip, so the search row carries a Cancel exit. Short unsearchable lists are content-height floating cards. The keyboard is simulated by writing the inset; the real one needs a device | mobile/dropdown-picker | ✅ |
| **Dropdowns only offer search from 16 options** (`SEARCH_MIN_OPTIONS`, `components/ui/dropdownSearch.js`, shared by `Select` and `MultiSelect`): shorter lists such as the 8 photo types fit one phone sheet, so they scroll with no keyboard; localities, floors and amenities keep search; `searchable`/`asyncSearch` still override | consumer/list-property/photo-ux, mobile/dropdown-picker, mobile/wizard-actions | ✅ |

## Shared UI components

Components used across many routes get their own row rather than being implied by
whichever route happens to render them — that is exactly how the picker
stylesheet bug below survived a 178-file suite.

| Component | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `DateField` / `TimeField` + their dialogs — styled, layered overlay on **every** route | platform/date-time-fields (desktop), mobile/date-time-fields (sheet) | ✅ |
| Ops/consumer forms use `DateField`, not a native date input: lead follow-up, KYC date of birth, registration/submission/issue dates | consumer/account/lead-notes, ops/identity-claim-qa, ops/registration-check, property-ownership-review | ✅ |
| `DualRange` manual entry — Cr/L/K parsing, junk input, Escape, bound ordering | consumer/search/dual-range-parsing | ✅ |
| `useCommitOnRelease` — a slider drag is **one** search, not one per step: nothing is fetched mid-drag, every readout still tracks the thumb while dragging, and exactly one `GET /properties` is issued when the value settles. Commit is the native `change` event, not a pointer gesture, so an arrow-key step searches too (the case that matters is an AT slider adjust, which fires no pointer or key event) — and because a range fires `change` on *every* keyboard step, holding the key is coalesced back to one search. Covers the budget `DualRange` and the near-a-place radius slider | consumer/search/filter-request-volume | ✅ |
| `lib/searchCache.js` — **a search already answered is not asked again**: switching sort away and back re-uses the remembered response and issues **no** request, while a third sort the cache has never held still reaches the server (the vacuity guard — "0 requests" is equally consistent with searching having been switched off). Red-checked at `ttl: 0`. The leave-the-route-and-come-back trip is deliberately *not* asserted: measured with the cache off it costs zero requests anyway, so the assertion would be green with the feature deleted | consumer/search/filter-request-volume | ✅ |
| `ConnectivityBanner` + `useConnectivity` + `LoadError` — **offline vs unreachable**: `setOffline(true)` raises the confident "You're offline" and coming back announces recovery then clears; a `page.route` abort while `navigator.onLine` stays **true** raises only the hedged "Can't reach the server"; a 500 answers so **no** banner is painted; a failed load offers a Retry instead of an empty state and the retry succeeds once the route is un-aborted; the live region is mounted while empty and announcing does not move focus (D165) | consumer/connectivity | ✅ |
| `ConnectivityBanner` + `useAppUpdate` + `BuildStampFilter` — **a tab that outlived its build**: rewriting `X-Draazy-Build` on the second `/api` answer raises "A new version is available" on the *fourth* banner state, which is pinned as **outranked but not discarded** — going offline with the offer already latched swaps the copy to "You're offline" (a reload on a dead uplink lands on a blank page), and the offer returns intact once the recovery notice clears. Nothing reloads by itself: a boot counter in `sessionStorage`, which unlike anything on `window` survives a reload, stays at **1** across the banner appearing and across a dismiss, because the wizard holds minutes of unsaved typing and installing a version nobody asked for trades a confusing 422 for a worse loss; and a dismissed build **stays** dismissed across a real in-app navigation that sends more traffic carrying it, since a rolling deploy drains the old version gradually and an unlatched banner would strobe for the length of every cutover. Vacuity is closed by a control that freezes the stamp and asserts **no** banner at all, plus a hit count that fails loudly if boot ever collapses to the single request the staging trick depends on. A guest `/listings` boot sends only one stamped read (reference reads are cached and unstamped, the page-view beacon waits 60s), so each test makes a client-side search change to send the second | consumer/app-update | ✅ |
| `ArticleFeedback` — positive/negative branches, persistence, per-article reset. The verdict also **leaves the browser**: `POST /help/feedback` carries the slug, the rendered language and `helpful`, claims no `comment` when none was typed, and is accepted (**202**, not merely dispatched). A thumbs-down reports **twice by design** — once bare the moment it is clicked, so a reader who never writes a reason still counts, and again with the prose — while the local record waits for the submit, so abandoning leaves the box reachable. The upload is fire-and-forget: aborting the route still thanks the reader and still writes the local record. The per-article reset is reached by **clicking** an in-app link, because a `goto` remounts the widget and would pass with the reset broken | platform/help/article-feedback | ✅ |


## Admin

| Route / Feature | Flow doc | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/admin` **RBAC nav** — which tabs an account may see, including manager atom-gated access, and which shell it may load at all | (all) | admin/consolidation, admin/rbac | ✅ |
| **The `/admin` scorecard is the server's, not the browser's** (D248) — this row used to read "`/admin` dashboard + RBAC nav ✅" and the dashboard half of that was false. `AdminDashboard.jsx` imported seven read functions straight from `lib/mockApi.js`, *below* the service seam, so on a live build eleven of fourteen tiles, both activity cards and the platform-health panel rendered numbers invented in the browser — and three side panels (Smart Alerts, SLA Health, Daily Ops Scorecard) were generated from a seeded PRNG with no server counterpart at all. Nothing failed: the two specs cited above pass because neither one reads a tile's value. The screen now reads `GET /admin/dashboard` → `AdminKpis` through `analyticsService.adminDashboard` (its `kpis` section), and the assertions are chosen to be ones the old code could not have satisfied. **Totals cannot be reached by paging** — `/users` serves 20 a page against 88 seeded users, so `88` is obtainable only from the aggregate and `20` is precisely what the regression looks like; the spec asserts `totalUsers > 20` first, because at a 20-row fixture the tile would stop discriminating and pass forever. **A fallback to mock is a `console.warn`, not an error**, so the mock seed's 62 users / 80 listings would have rendered a confident wrong page — the live figures differ from it in every asserted field, which is what makes a silent fallback fail here. Two contract drifts were caught in the repoint rather than shipped: `traffic[]` rows carry `sessions`, not the `visits` the screen read (a naive repoint renders `0` with full confidence), and ticket status `new` does not exist server-side (`open`/`in-progress`/`waiting`/`resolved`/`closed`), which `ticketMapper` deliberately does not translate. `revenue30d` is asserted on both halves of its redaction — non-null for an admin, `null` for staff, every other field identical — because `fmtINR(null)` prints ₹0, so a redaction the screen fails to honour reads as a real figure of zero | (all) | admin/admin-dashboard | ✅ |
| **An ops account is moved to the staff portal, never served the admin console** — `/admin/properties` lands a staffer on `/staff/properties` (no Settings or Team links), and `/admin` lands her on `/staff` with her "My work" page. `GET /admin/dashboard` redacts per section, so a staffer without the `analytics` function gets no `kpis`/`traffic`/`sla` section (absent, never zero); one holding it is served the scorecard with `revenue30d` null (finance is admin-only) and every other field equal to the admin's. Pinned together deliberately: the API is the security boundary and the client guard is the thing that must already be correct | (all) | admin/admin-dashboard, admin/rbac | ✅ |
| **The `/admin` queues underneath the scorecard are the server's too** (D249) — D248 fixed the tiles and left the reads beneath them alone. `AdminDashboard` fetched `listForModeration({}, 'newest')` — a hundred-row cap over the newest listings in every status — and derived from that one array the Flagged tile, the stale/awaiting-owner follow-up lists and the Pending verification card. Every derivation was wrong past the cap, and in the direction that hides work: a browser-side `filter(status === 'flagged').length` can only ever move *downwards* as the catalogue grows (the same mistake that once painted `Active 0` over 54 approved listings), and looking for the listings that have waited *longest* inside a page of the *newest* rows discards precisely the rows the card exists to surface — the stalest pending listing is the last thing a newest-first page keeps. The queue, its counts (pending, flagged, follow-up) and the five oldest rows now come from the `listings` section of `GET /admin/dashboard` as whole-set COUNT queries; the follow-up tile used to be a capped client union, and New Enquiries / Deals in Progress filtered on statuses that do not exist (`new`, `in_progress`) so always read 0 — they now count `pending` contact requests and deals not `closed`. Both new claims are written so the old code fails them without needing a hundred-listing database, which no test may assume: **the queue card's five rows are asserted in order against an independent `GET /admin/properties?status=pending&archived=false&sort=createdAt,asc&size=5`, behind a premise guard that fails unless the ascending and descending heads differ** — so it cannot go vacuously green on a small seed, and `PropertySort` appends `id desc` as a total tiebreaker so the comparison is deterministic rather than planner-dependent. **The Flagged tile is pinned by where its number comes from, not by the number** — inside the page cap the old browser filter and the server's count agree, so the discriminating assertion is that serving `/admin/dashboard` without its `listings` section takes the tile off the page entirely, which a screen that zeroed a missing section cannot do, beside a Pending Verification tile asserted equal to `GET /admin/properties/summary`; a still-present `Pending Verification` tile beside it is what stops that from being satisfied by a page that failed to render | (all) | admin/admin-dashboard | ✅ |
| **A staffer's dashboard has a "Waiting for you" tile for every queue function they hold, and for no other** — `/admin/my-work` computes a queue only for the caller's effective functions; Reviews (pending), Enquiries (pending contact requests), Societies (member-added, not merged/archived), Referrals (pending/qualified) are SQL branches beside the existing kyc/support/reports/desk ones, and Flatmates reuses `FlatmateModerationService.moderationSummary()` so the tile equals the moderation desk's Pending tab (no oldest age, so it reads "Waiting"). Scoped to `reports`+`referrals`+`societies`, the API returns exactly those three queues and the page shows three tiles linking to `/staff/...`, none for Enquiries, Flatmates or Review moderation | `/staff`, `/admin/my-work` | admin/admin-dashboard | ✅ |
| **Admin topbar palette + notifications** — Ctrl+K navigates real admin pages everywhere, and listings and people are searched over the seam (`propertyService.searchForModeration`, `usersService.listUsers`) so the same four categories answer in every build; the bell is one slim `GET /admin/bell` (totals plus five rows per queue; a queue the caller may read but the server left out is named rather than printed as "All caught up.", one the caller may not read is simply absent) Tickets, enquiries and deals were dropped: their list endpoints carry no `q`, so they could only ever have been answered from the browser's demo store. On a phone the search icon reveals the box and the palette answers. | (all) | admin/command-palette | ✅ |
| **The live suite runs against its own persistent database, `draazy_e2e`** — not `draazy` (a run would wipe dev work) and not `draazy_test` (the Java suite asserts that one is empty). Rows **commit and survive a backend restart**, which is the point: a user registered by a spec is still there after the JVM is killed, proven directly (`9700009911` → `a768c4d0-…`, identical id across a restart). Drift is controlled by resetting to baseline at **run start** rather than teardown — a teardown only runs if the run reaches it, and it destroys the evidence in exactly the crash case you wanted it. `global-setup.live.js` truncates every table it discovers from `pg_tables` except `flyway_schema_history` (discovered, not hand-listed: a stale list fails silently) and re-applies the three seeds in Flyway order. Idempotent — consecutive runs both settle at 88 users / 39 properties | — | e2e/global-setup.live.js, e2e/scripts/reset-e2e-db.sql | ✅ |
| **Live sign-in is a real OTP round trip, not a log scrape** — the `e2e` profile pins the code so specs stop reading it out of the backend log, but issuance, hashing and `otp_codes` are untouched, so the flow is genuine and still fails the way it should. Proven in four steps: replaying a used code → 401 *"No active OTP"*, a fresh request → 200 `otpSent`, a wrong code → 401 *"Incorrect OTP"*, the fixed code → 200 authenticated. Three independent guards keep it out of production — the property defaults empty, `application-prod.properties` pins it empty, and `@PostConstruct` refuses to start the context if a non-empty code survives into a production profile | — | e2e/helpers/liveAuth.js, backend `OtpService` | ✅ |
| **Restoring an archived staff account whose email address has since been re-used is refused, and the refusal is not cosmetic** — a guard that answers 409 and restores anyway is the original defect wearing an error message, and it would pass every other assertion. Also covers: an address with no live claimant still restores, and staff login for the contested address answers **401, not 500** — D206 removed the password parameter so no credential can succeed, but 401-vs-500 still separates "resolved to one row and rejected" from "matched two rows again" | — | admin/user-restore-email-collision | ✅ |

| **The flatmates board's counts and paging are the server's, not the page's** (D263) — the header counted the cards it was holding and the board had no pager at all, because it read one 200-row window and narrowed it in the browser: a 201st room was both unreachable and unmentioned, and "12 homes available" was a claim about a window dressed as a claim about Pune. `/flatmates/feed` now takes the full facet set and answers `totalElements`/`verifiedElements` counted over the whole set, plus `otherTabElements` for the other tab's badge, so a page load is one feed request. The page totals are **staged by an intercept**, because the lane seeds fewer rows than one page — on real data `totalElements === content.length`, so a header reading either would pass and the test would prove nothing. The intercept rewrites only the two counts and pins the upstream page index (asking the real server for page 1 of a 12-row set correctly returns nothing, and the pager under test disappears mid-assertion); `content` and the outgoing request are untouched, so the click on Page 2 is asserted as `page=1` on the wire — which is also where the 1-based control meets the 0-based API. Verified-only is asserted **without** staging, landing on exactly the verified count the header showed before it was pressed: the one number a client-side count could never be checked against. The empty case types into the search field rather than passing `?q=` (the board hydrates `loc`/`g`/`near*`/`view`/`post` from the URL, not `q`) and rather than pressing Enter, which hands the text to the smart-search parser instead of the `q` facet. The dev response is the relative `/api/dev/storage/public/...` route, then the gallery must visibly render that server URL rather than a local copy kept beside it. The key must have the `photos/<16-hex tag>/<uuid>` shape and must not contain the uploader's user id: `PhotoKeys` derives that tag with an HMAC, so a public URL never links photos to a person. No signed-out visitor has that document, so **live, every price Draazy quoted came from the constant** — and an operator who changed a price in the back office was told it saved, and it did save, and nothing read it. The same seven numbers lived in the bundle and in the seed row, and while the browser never asked the server the two could not contradict each other, so they had drifted apart on five of the seven with nothing failing. Both tests are built around the fact that this failure is **designed to be invisible**: `PricingProvider` seeds its state with `PRICING_DEFAULTS` so first paint is never blank, which means a browser that never issues the request renders exactly what a successful one does. So the page test *waits on the response* — a silent failure times out rather than passing — and asserts the rendered figures against **what that response carried**, never against a literal, so the spec cannot rot into a second copy of the price list. | **A page that quotes no price does not fetch the price list; the page that does asks once** — the other half of the same contract. `PricingProvider` is mounted by `ConsumerLayout` on every consumer route, but only five route-level screens render a figure from it, so an eager fetch on mount spent a request on the home page, on search and on every property detail. Both halves assert a **count**, not a wait: "the home page did not fetch" and "the home page fetched twice" are equally consistent with any assertion that merely waits for an event, and `/plans` fetching *exactly once* is the claim worth defending — the provider, not the hook, is what dedupes, so a regression that loses `activate`'s referential stability, or lets a second priced component force a re-run, slips past a `toBeGreaterThan(0)`. The `/plans` half is not decoration either: a zero on the home page is equally consistent with the fetch having been **deleted** rather than deferred, and a deleted fetch is the original bug above (every price quoted from the bundle) coming back wearing an optimisation as a disguise. The `/plans` request is armed with `waitForRequest` *before* the navigation — it sits several hops behind the paint (consumer effect → `setActive` → provider re-render → provider effect → an `await` on the provider resolver), so a locator wait can win the race and assert on a count about to become 1. Waits on `appReady()` rather than `networkidle`, which on this app resolves against an empty document. What it does not cover is the client-side transition, where the only link to `/plans` lives in a collapsible drawer; the uncovered part there is a first-paint window, which is a rendering question rather than a request-count one | plans | fees-and-photos | ✅ |

Counts are asserted as a shape rather than a magnitude — always numbers, never null, never negative — with two containment rules (`breachedCount ≤ completedCount`, `outstandingBreachingCount ≤ outstandingCount`) that catch the two halves being counted over different sets, and `slaRatePct` reconciled against the counts it is derived from so it cannot drift into being a third independent number. **Both** routes refused **403** for a plain signed-in consumer — they carry separate copies of the same guard expression, so each is checked. The moderator opening the case file to decide saw a blank panel and rang the owner a second time. Proven by HTTP before anything was changed: a POST under each id, then a GET under each, returning one row apiece. The near-miss that decided the fix: `PropertyReviewModal` already declares `const pid = (listing) => listing?.uuid || listing?.id` and uses it for three other calls, and `listNotes` three lines below does not — client-side discipline had already been written down and had already failed, so canonicalisation belongs on the server, following the `ReviewTargetKey` precedent of accepting whatever public identifier the caller holds and storing the key the schema uses. `NoteEntityKey` resolves a non-uuid property id through `findBySlug` — which carries no `archived` filter deliberately, because V90 made `entity_id` a bare text column precisely so a note about a listing archived an hour later survives — and `V123` merges the histories already written. **Why no suite could have caught this.** Every mock spec speaks slug on both sides of every assertion and `notes` spoke uuid on both sides; each was self-consistent, and one localStorage database answering to whichever id it is handed makes the fake agree with either. The assertion had to be one that *crosses*: file under the uuid in the enquiries board's own shape (`action: 'responded'`), file under the slug in the console's, then require **both texts** back from **both** reads. Mutation-proven by returning the id unresolved — the crossing test fails on exactly the missing note and nothing else does. A second test pins that normalising did not quietly become existence-checking: an id matching no listing still takes a note, because refusing one would lose the note about the listing somebody just archived. The comms-log timeline test moved here from the mock in the same change, since "the note is rendered on the log" needs the row to come back on a server read; it uses a **freshly created** listing, which has no slug at all — nothing in the codebase mints property slugs outside the seed — so it is stable across the approval it performs, while the crossing test uses a seeded listing and only adds rows, leaving the queue counts `properties-console` asserts untouched | property-verification | admin/notes | ✅ |

Two assertions used to pin known-wrong behaviour so that fixing it would be a visible change; one of them has now been fixed and its test rewritten. **`wa-pricing` quotes the locality's published rate** (`localities.rate_per_sqft` — 11200 for Kothrud; the mock's invented `9,500`-for-all-of-Pune is what was wrong, not the act of quoting a number, and the 140 localities with no published rate still render `{market_rate}` literally so the staff member can see there is none). 'wa-dormant' : 'wa-stale')` — which makes *what to say to this owner* an inference the browser draws, on a board whose membership the **server** decided, from a different input. `unconfirmed=true` is answered by `Freshness.of(lastConfirmedAt, createdAt, now)`; `freshnessState` reads `freshenedAt || createdAt`, and `freshenedAt` is `propertyMapper`'s name for `lastConfirmedAt`, which `GET /admin/properties` does not emit — it ships the verdict (`freshness`) and not the timestamp behind it, so the browser re-derives the tier from the posting date. Scoped by uuid rather than title, because seeded titles are formulaic enough that several rows answer to one and "click the first reminder button" would send a real chaser to whichever owner sorted first. | **The Directory tab paging off the server** — the last tab on this console that still read the bundled `data/societies.js`, and the tell was on the KPI beside it: "Societies" counted the array the tab had in hand, so the moment that array became one server page the tile would have read **20** and called it the platform's society count. The catalogue is 348 rows shipped as a 182 KB lazy chunk, which is why the old tab also had a completeness gate (`useSocietyCatalogue`) — a client-side pager over a list the client does not fully have yet paints 28 of 348 and, without a re-render, never corrects itself. Now `listSocietyDirectory` is a seam function on both providers: the http side returns `GET /societies`'s page envelope, the mock side awaits `ensureSocietyCatalogue()` and slices **for real**, because a mock that returned all 348 in one go would let a broken "Next" pass — page two would already be on screen. This test asserts the count line (`1–20 of N`), then clicks Next and asserts **both** that the line reads `21–40` **and** that the first cell changed, since a pager that advances its label while re-rendering the same rows is the exact bug the label hides. Then it searches and asserts the matching row appears — the search box is not a nicety here but the compensation for paging: twenty at a time over 350 rows is eighteen clicks to reach a known building, so server-side paging without server-side filtering trades one usability defect for another (D129). **Now live, and the conversion is the row's whole point.** A mock spec cannot separate the two implementations it exists to distinguish: `Table` carries an internal pager that slices rows already in the browser, and against a fixture holding the whole catalogue a client-side slice and a server round trip render the same range, the same rows and the same changed first cell. So the live file asserts the **outbound request** (`page=1` leaving the browser) rather than the rendering, and searches for a society read from the catalogue's *last* page — a target the loaded twenty provably do not contain, so a client-side filter would render the perfectly calm "No societies match that search" instead of failing. Replaces the mock `societies.spec.js`, whose other five tests either had live homes already (`society-admin` for the overlay, `societies` for the candidates queue) or were false against a seeded database — its "empty claims queue" and "empty moderation queues" both asserted an emptiness the seed does not have, the latter with no positive anchor at all | content-localities-societies | admin/societies-directory | ✅ |

That bug is invisible to a mock spec **by construction** — a seeded spec asserts against the same `localStorage` the dialog wrote, so it passes *because* the write never left the browser, and the mock `societies.spec.js` was green throughout — that file has since been deleted for the same reason. | `/admin/flatmates` — **retired**; the route is now the live desk itself, and the retired `/ops/flatmate-review` bookmark redirects to it (and is refused with the module's tab switched off). The admin desk moderated seekers, groups and group applications out of `db.json`, could not see rooms at all, and had no view of the D72 publication axis, so the three queues it offered were a subset of the live desk's three read against a store nothing else wrote. What is left is the redirect and its two guards: an unauthenticated visitor is still sent to `/staff-login` rather than through to the ops desk, and a buyer is still refused. **`flatmate-moderation-reach.spec.js` has since been retired** — what it covered was the consumer board honouring D72's publication rule, and its docblock had described that rule backwards for as long as it existed: it claimed the server filters `mod_status not in ('flagged','removed','rejected')` and that the mock mirrors it via `MOD_HIDDEN`. Both halves are false. `FlatmateRoomRepository` filters `r.modStatus in ('live','approved')` — an **allow-list** — and there is no `MOD_HIDDEN` anywhere in the frontend; the export is `MOD_PUBLIC`. The code was right and the prose was wrong, which is the worse way round. It also had teeth: a blacklist and a whitelist agree on `flagged`, `removed` and `rejected` and disagree on `pending`, the state every new post is born in, so the file's two absence tests could not fail for the one regression the design exists to prevent — proved by rewriting `isPubliclyVisible` as the blacklist its own source comment warns against, which left both green (3 passed) and reddened only the **new third test**, `an unreviewed room is hidden`. That asymmetry is the point: `removed` leaking means one post a moderator wanted gone is still up, `pending` leaking means every unreviewed post in the city is public by default — and it fails silently, because a board full of posts is what a working board looks like. A fourth correction: the control test was titled "a removed room disappears from the public flatmates board" while seeding no verdict and asserting the room **is** visible, so a list-reporter run of this file appeared to cover removal twice and the visible-by-default case not at all. | flatmates | ops/flatmate-moderation | ✅ |
