Here the flag is turned off through `PUT /admin/settings` and turned back on in a `finally`, with the restore proved through the screen rather than through the response — the read path is a different one (`AdminFlagsContext` merges the document over its defaults), and a restore that satisfied the API while leaving the console dark is exactly the leak the block exists to avoid. The fallback is asserted with its way out (an "Enable in Settings" link, because a dead end means an administrator being told by somebody else which of forty switches to look for) and against a control: the feed's table and its `kpi-total` tile must be **gone**, since every positive assertion above is also satisfied by a page that simply failed to load — the same pixels a broken fetch produces Performance/Activity log are underline `role=tab` tabs; Back-office/All actors is a chip group. | settings-team-staff | admin/staff-activity | ✅ |

## Ops

| Route / Feature | Flow doc | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/ops/flatmate-review` — the flatmate desk, live-only. **One queue, three tabs, one popup:** Pending (default, newest first) merges posts/rooms/groups awaiting publish, published rows edited since review, pending badge claims and pending group applications into one card list (a room both awaiting publish and claiming a badge is one card with both reason chips), then Published and Hidden & removed. Every decision is taken in the **Review flatmate post** popup, which loads `GET /admin/flatmates/{id}` (photos, full untruncated free text, a details grid, full host mobile, an **Edit details** form for headline, description, rent/budget, deposit, localities and move-in (`PATCH /admin/flatmates/{id}`, audited, no re-review), re-check, Badge verification, Decision). The old Verification / Moderation / Group applications boards and their sub-tabs are gone; the history below describes what they asserted, and every claim now runs through the popup. The mock modelled **one third** of it: `lib/data/flatmates.js` knew host verification and nothing at all about the D72 publication axis or about group applications, so two of the three boards had never been exercised by a test. Converting it also meant building something, not just wiring: the group-applications board read a table nothing in the product could write to, so `POST /flatmates/groups/{id}/apply` plus an owner inbox (`GET`/`PATCH /me/group-applications`) and `GET /me/flatmate-groups` were added — four routes the OpenAPI contract does not name, an intentional extension recorded in `docs/flows/ops/flatmate-moderation.md` section 10. Two rulings are visible on screen: the host's mobile is shown in full to the back office, and the moderation axis offers no `rejected` because on that axis it means what `removed` means. Nothing on the desk says the bare word **flag**: `flag_for_review` is a guardrail the server raises and `mod_status = 'flagged'` is this desk's verdict, two columns that shared one word across two boards, so both the moderation and the applications board now say **Hide for review** and **Hidden for review** and the verification board says **Contested address** (D9). Live coverage (9 tests, one pinning the three-tab layout): a tenant declaration reaches Verification and approving moves the **badge and not the post** (the two axes must not reach each other); a rejection without a reason is refused; a brand-new seeker post is born Pending and is absent from the public feed until Publish, which is the whole claim D72 makes; Remove takes a note and the free text is rendered untruncated (it is where a phone number goes when the contact field will not take one); and the group-applications board shows a **real** row for the first time. Two more arrived with the route retirements: the uploaded agreement is openable from its row while a row without one says so, and the retired `/admin/flatmates` hands the operator to this desk without laundering a switched-off module onto it (M9). The eighth is the **Re-check** board (D5): a published tenant-tier room has every photo replaced and *stays up* while landing on a board of its own, named with the field that moved, because the edit path used to re-run only the trust ladder — and the ladder knows who the host is, not what they changed, so an approved gallery could be swapped wholesale for one no moderator had ever seen. Its thumbnails are rendered on the row (D6): a number burned into an image, or a photo lifted from another listing, is invisible to a desk that renders only text, and the queue previously carried a headline and a locality and nothing to judge. The same test asserts a second room, edited in the same run through the same shape of request but changing only a chip-list facet, is **not** there — a queue that also fills with furnishing changes is one a moderator learns to skim, and a skimmed queue is no queue — and that deciding the row clears it, since a human has now read the room, which is the whole of what the work item asked. The swap is driven over the API because `PATCH /flatmates/rooms/{id}` has no host screen at all (the product exposes only `/seats` and `/occupants`); every assertion is made against the rendered board, which is the seam that exists. All the fixtures are minted through the consumer path, because the seed's five flatmate rows are all already `approved`. **M11 deleted the three seeded consumer cues that were still in `flatmate-review.spec.js`** — not converted, deleted, because `consumer/flatmates/review-status` already owns all three labels and owns them far more strongly: a group waiting on Ops says so and is refused the badge it asked for, an Ops approval turns that same group Tenant-verified, and a rejected group stays on the board saying the review failed — each posted over HTTP, moderated through the real queue, and only then read off the card, so the label is proved **earned** rather than rendered. What was here planted rows into `draazyFlatmateReviews` and asserted the renderer against them, which also asserted something the browser cannot know: that map is written to one reviewer's browser and is empty on every other machine, so the badge it proved was a claim about `localStorage`, not about a verification. The offline panel is the whole of the file now, and is the one claim only mock mode can make — which is why it went when mock mode did (`flatmate-review.spec.js`, retired D256). The shut panel it asserted cannot be reached any more: with every domain on http there is no build that renders it | flatmate-moderation | ops/flatmate-moderation, consumer/flatmates/review-status | ✅ |
| `/ops/flatmate-review` type tabs — **All · Room · Group · Seeker post** under every status tab, filtering the loaded queue in the browser; group applications sit under Group, and the chosen type survives a status switch. A property-queue style toolbar — search (title, author, locality, note), All/Today/7d/30d date pills, every tab newest first with no sort toggle — with "Nothing matches these filters." when the filters, not the queue, are empty | flatmate-moderation | ops/flatmate-moderation | ✅ |
| Flatmate review popup shows the evidence: "View agreement" opens the uploaded `data:` agreement as a same-origin `blob:` (Chromium refuses a top-frame navigation to `data:`, so the tab used to stay blank), and uploaded photos served from a root-relative `/api/dev/storage/...` path render instead of being dropped as non-http | flatmate-moderation | ops/flatmate-moderation | ✅ |
| A room or group its host deleted (archived) leaves the Ops desk entirely. The publish axis already filtered `archived = false`, but the badge-claim queue (`FlatmateReviewRepository.findForQueue`) read `flatmate_reviews` with no check on its target, so a deleted tenant room lingered on Pending as a "Badge claim · tenant" card with no status, photos or locality. The query now skips reviews whose room or group is archived, which also clears rows deleted before the fix. Server: `FlatmateSupplyEndpointsTest.deletedRoomLeavesTheQueue` | flatmate-moderation | ops/flatmate-moderation | ✅ |
| A group applies for a whole flat — the loop the mock never had. `lib/groupApplications.js` seeded two rows into `localStorage` and offered an owner two buttons; nothing could create an application, so the board it fed could never have a row. The card on the listing page appears only for a signed-in visitor who hosts a live group with an open seat, on somebody else's **rental** listing (a sale price is whole consideration, so per-head would be wrong by orders of magnitude). The owner answers from the dashboard Action Center, writing `status` — an axis the ops desk deliberately cannot reach, since it writes `modStatus`. Server rules are pinned in `FlatmateApplicationEndpointsTest` (13 tests), including that a stranger's `PATCH` gets **404 not 403**, to avoid an existence oracle | flatmate-moderation | consumer/flatmates/group-apply | ✅ |
| Flatmate hold-for-review (V75): only an unflagged **owner-tier** room or group self-publishes; every tenant/identity-tier post is born `pending` and stays off the public board until Ops presses Publish (`approved`). A badge approval does not publish: the merged card keeps "Awaiting publish" after the badge is decided, and only Publish moves it to Published and onto the board. A moderator's `live` verdict is stored as `approved`, so `live` always means owner-tier self-publish and an edit that drops the tier takes it down, while an approved post survives non-foundation edits. Server rules in `FlatmateSupplyEndpointsTest` (`tenantTierIsBornPending`, `losingOwnerTierLosesTheSelfPublish`) and `FlatmateEditAndInterestEndpointsTest` (`aTenantTierPostWaits`) | flatmate-moderation | ops/flatmate-moderation, consumer/flatmates/trust-badges, consumer/flatmates/terms-and-occupancy | ✅ |
| **Flatmate maker-checker at the API.** A staffer gets 403 moderating, desk-editing, badging or deciding an application on an item they authored or whose group they belong to, and 403 joining a group, sending interest or splitting a flat; Flag/Remove need a note (400), and so does publishing a removed item; a repeat verdict is a no-op 200; an `expired` item takes only Remove (other verdicts 409) and renewing it then keeps it removed; a decided badge review answers a different verdict with 409; a pending room, or one whose author is archived, cannot be saved or opened (404); an ask, a seeker post's move-in text or a split room note carrying a phone number is a 422; a stranger editing someone's post gets 404; accepting on a deleted post, an application from a group that is no longer live (archived, taken down or its host suspended), or restoring a removed accepted application once another group holds the flat or its own group is gone is a 409; erasure drops pending asks sent and received and the pending group applications the erased user made. Server rules in `FlatmateMakerCheckerTest` (29 tests), and stale writes (application verdict vs owner accept, badge vs takedown, renew vs takedown, owner consent vs takedown, unsplit vs a new occupant) in `FlatmateStaleWriteTest` | flatmate-moderation | ops/flatmate-maker-checker | ✅ |
| **The desk's Decision section enforces the note.** Remove and Hide for review stay disabled until a note is typed (Publish on a removed item too); an `expired` item lists under Hidden & removed and offers only Remove (note required) beside "Expired — only the host's renewal brings it back."; Publish, Hide for review and Looks fine are absent, and Remove lands `mod_status = removed` | flatmate-moderation | ops/flatmate-moderation ("removing a post needs a note…", "an expired post can only be removed from the desk, with a note") | ✅ |

| `/admin/home-loans` - the Home Loans ticket board, live-only. `/ops/requests` is not a route (the `/ops/*` catch-all applies), so the staff ticket-board coverage uses the loans desk page. Converting it off `lib/mockApi.js` was not wiring: the mock knew **three** statuses where `TicketStatuses` knows five, assigned by display **name** where `TicketUpdate` takes a user **id**, and overwrote notes instead of appending. Live coverage (7 tests): a customer-raised ticket reaches the loans desk; the status tabs carry the server words and not `New`/`Done` (the tab list and the drawer offer all five); a claim (the row's Claim button, self-claim) writes the caller's id and resolved name; a claim does not advance the ticket; notes append (via the drawer's Save) and survive reload; and a loans staffer cannot see a legal ticket that provably exists. | service-queues | ops/ops-board | ✅ |
| The verification case file (D218), live-only. Three things the mock cannot hold, because in mock mode none of them exist. **(1)** A second listing on one electricity meter is published, not refused — sometimes it is a broker relisting somebody's flat and sometimes a genuine re-let, and refusing outright punishes the honest case to catch the dishonest one — but it files a staff-only note naming the incumbent listing. The note deliberately does **not** quote the meter number, and that is asserted: `review_messages.body` is free text sitting outside the `PrivateFieldVisibility` projection and outside the erasure classification, so a note carrying the value would route a guarded field around its own guard where no mapper test would look (D219 review). The owner asking for their own case file gets **404, not an empty thread**: an empty thread still tells them a file was opened on them. **(2)** The stays-live re-check note is now composed **server-side**; until D218 `PropertyReviewModal` typed it in the browser, so an edit through any other client produced silence and the note claimed "approved" whether or not the write had landed. **(3)** A **pending** listing is not told it stays live and gets no case file manufactured for it — `requestRecheck` refuses on anything not publicly visible, so the note had been narrating work nobody was doing. Fixtures are minted on the wire through `POST /me/listings` rather than through the wizard, which keeps the fixture independent of the UI even now that the wizard reaches the same endpoint (D219). Server rules are pinned in `ListingNoticesTest` (19 tests) | property | ops/verification-thread | ✅ |
| Who may **read** and who may **decide** a verification case (D219), live-only and deliberately a separate file from the thread spec above: a broken workflow is a bug report, a broken refusal is a disclosure, and a red line should say which just happened. Four tests. A signed-in stranger gets **404** on every thread route — read, post, mark-read and open — because a 403 would confirm that a listing with that id exists and is under review, which is the fact a competitor walking ids would probe for; the owner and the desk are asserted to get 200 on the same case first, so the refusals cannot be explained away as "the case was never created". The **bodies** of the two 404s are compared as well as the codes, since "no such listing" and "not yours" as distinguishable replies would restore the oracle the status code was chosen to remove. The staff-only routes answer **403** instead, and that is not an inconsistency: their guard is a role rather than a relationship, `@PreAuthorize` refuses before the id is ever looked up, so nothing about the row can leak through a method that was never entered. Last, the maker-checker rule, which is the only refusal here that is about neither: a staffer listing their own flat is a participant **and** holds `properties:write`, so every other guard passes and the listing would publish with nobody having read it — a second staffer decides the same case without complaint, which is what separates "the rule works" from "the route is broken" | property | ops/verification-access | ✅ |
| **Posting on behalf makes you the maker** (RC QA MC-01/02/03). An admin who posts via `POST /admin/properties` gets 403 on the admin correction (`PATCH /admin`), on recording ownership evidence, on granting the ownership badge and on `/status`; a peer then rejects. A second verdict that omits `expectedStatus` on a non-pending listing gets 409 `stale_decision`, so two racing staff cannot both win. Duplicate merge/dismiss refusals for the maker, and the 404 for an unknown keeper, are pinned in `DuplicateMergeOwnerNoticeTest` | property | ops/verification-access — *the staffer who posted on behalf is a maker everywhere* | ✅ |

| `/ops/referrals` - the **referral audit** desk, live-only. Rewards are automatic now (low risk is granted, medium is held for `referralHoldDays`, high is rejected `high_risk`), so the desk audits, overrides and claws back rather than approving every row. The desk opens on **Held** (tabs Held / Pending = pending only / High risk / Rewarded = qualified + rewarded / Refused / All, each with a count; action buttons disable while a request is in flight); a row carries the status and risk chips, its track (Owner / Seeker / Contacts-only), "Held until", the "Auto reason" (Needs review for `medium_risk`, High risk, Cap reached, Self-dealing, Referee suspended, ...) and the four signal chips. Live coverage: a redeemed referral reaches Pending with both numbers in full; a medium-risk qualification is **held** and shows on the default tab with its release day while the referrer sees "checking" and nothing spendable; approving a held row releases the reward the referrer can spend (+15 contacts); a high-risk one is auto-rejected with its reason and offers only **Appeal: approve**; an appeal needs a reason on the server (422 for none, blank or `{}`) and on the desk (cancelling or leaving it blank writes nothing); approve no longer needs the referee to hold an identity badge; a clawback reads `clawed-back`, not `rejected`; **High risk** is a `risk` filter with no Flagged tab; a buyer's token gets 403. Every test mints its own referrer and referee through the consumer path and sets the risk band with SQL, because redeem derives it from loopback signals that are identical for every test | referrals-fraud | ops/referrals | ✅ |
| `/ops/support` — the admin support queue, live-only. Properties-style layout: underline tabs Awaiting reply / Answered / All, each with its server total (one-row reads, so the pills describe the whole queue rather than the page on screen); one panel with a note per tab, Refresh and a server pager in the toolbar; row cards carry the status badge, a "Waiting on us" / "Unopened by customer" chip, raiser · category · opened, and a Reply (awaiting) / View thread action. The mock could state the queue but not the thing the queue is *for*: one store and one flag meant "the desk read it" and "the customer read it" were the same bit, so the two-sided read model (D50/V53) — the whole reason `staff_unread` exists as a second column — was unfalsifiable against it. Live, opening a ticket clears the desk's side and is asserted to leave the customer's alone, read back through the raiser's own `GET /support/tickets/{id}`. The conversion also found a 500: `SupportTicketMapper` dereferenced a **nullable** `author_id` three lines below code that filters nulls out of the name lookup, so one message whose author had gone took the entire thread down for the person who raised it | service-queues | ops/support-queue | ✅ |

| **Only the holder shares a draft** — a colleague who has not taken the matter gets a 409 naming the holder, and the holder's share then succeeds. Starting work on an unheld request (`new → in-progress`) takes it, so an approved matter always has someone to upload its registered copy (`ServiceRequestFlowTest` — *starting work takes the request*, *only the holder shares the draft*) | service-queues / maker-checker | ops/drafting-desk — *only the operator holding a matter can share its draft* | ✅ |
| **Registration check** (`RegistrationCheck`, completed rentals) — the registered copy is what makes a tenancy row, and a *second* operator is what makes it evidence. The final upload prepares one `rent_agreements` row per tenant (`draft`, linked to the request and the uploaded document, V39) from mobiles the server derives rather than a desk types — and none for a flat that was never listed, which still completes (there is no listing to badge); the uploader's own `PATCH /admin/rent-agreements/{id}` to `registered` is a 403 even for an admin, and a rental-desk colleague confirms it from the desk UI. Confirming is its own grant, `registrations:write`, which ships inside the `desk:rental` function: a colleague scoped to every other desk but not Rent Agreement gets 403 on the PATCH and no door to the desk (the "needs the registration-check permission" note is unreachable by staff and is not asserted). Only an OTP-held tenant number (the non-owner requester or an accepted co-fill invitee) can be confirmed: a form-typed tenant shows the "typed on the form" note, has no *Confirm* button, and a forced PATCH is 422. The desk shows the mobile in full, opens the exact copy the row was prepared from, and the row's status comes back from the server, not the click; a customer's own `final-document` upload on the closed matter is refused. Settled in Postgres rather than by a signed webhook: the machine's `.env.local` may carry a real Cashfree secret that a spec must never hold, and the payment gate is `ServiceRequestFlowTest`'s to prove. Backend: `RentAgreementRegistrationTest` (desk scoping, signatory refusal, a staff requester cannot work his own matter but can approve it as customer, an admin listing owner is refused, only the holder uploads the final copy, a customer cannot file `draft`/`final-document` or upload once closed, a typed-in tenant, licensor, co-owner or witness on staff is refused, a stranger's listing prepares no rows, a never-listed flat completes and prepares none, invite-mode leftovers and invalid mobiles are dropped, and the tenant sees the row only once registered; typed tenants 422, tenant-requester and invitee registrable, lookup normalises `+91` mobiles, a maker scoped to `services:write` alone is 403 and a `registrations:write` checker 200) and `AgreementsAndKycTest` (`POST /me/rent-agreements` retired → 405, unlinked rows admin-only, `registered` without evidence 422, a tenant sees an unlinked row only once registered and never its owner-typed URL) | service-queues / flatmate trust | ops/registration-check | ✅ |
| **Registration record** (`StaffWorkflowActions` final form, `RegistrationRecord`) — a rent agreement completes only with what the Sub-Registrar and GRAS gave it: document number, SRO, date, challan GRN, stamp duty and fee paid (V41). A bare `final-doc` is 422 naming the missing fields and the request stays `approved`; the desk keeps *Upload registered copy* disabled until all six are typed; a lowercase GRN is accepted and stored upper-case; after upload the checker's panel shows the record and flags "Stamp duty paid ₹1 differs from" the quoted duty while an equal fee raises nothing. A valuation's final form shows none of the fields. Backend: `RentAgreementRegistrationTest.registrationRecordIsRequiredAndShown` (422 lists every bad field and only those — future date, non-`MH` GRN; paid and quoted on the read; reused GRN in another case → 409; same SRO case-insensitively + document no. + year → 409) | service-queues | ops/registration-check | ✅ |

| **Re-priced terms (amendment)** (`AmendTerms` on the desk, `RevisedTerms` on the tracker). The holder revises rent from ₹20,000 to ₹25,000 with a reason. No tenant OTP step (tenant consent retired). Locally, a mock checkout offers a confirm that calls the `@LocalOnly` `POST /service-requests/{id}/payment/simulate`. Backend: `ServiceRequestFlowTest.PaidGate` (`invisibleToTheQueueUntilPaid`, `localPaymentSimulation`), `RentAgreementReadinessTest`. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement-submit ("the owner pays before anything reaches the team…") | 🟡 |
| **Every executing party approves the current draft** (MC-4, V48). Account holders approve in-app; inline parties approve by OTP. Witnesses are not asked. A rejection sends the request to changes-requested, and a new version resets every approval. Backend `RentAgreementDraftApprovalTest`. | service-queues | ops/registration-check (`approvedAgreement` helper has the co-fill tenant approve) | ✅ |
| **A risky draft waits for a second operator** (MC-8, D-i). A draft with rent ≥ ₹50k, a co-owner or POA, an NRI/foreign party or an overlap stays hidden from the customer until a different `services:write` operator releases it or sends it back; the maker cannot release their own. A revision shared while the request is already draft-shared is held the same way: the customer, and any staff member who is the requester or an accepted party, sees no draft, no `draftCheck`, no `draftApproval` and no `draft.check-*` events, and opened / decision / OTP are 409 until release. A sent-back revision stays hidden the same way, and stays hidden even after a later revision is released; after a decision the customer still gets no `draftCheck` (reasons and notes are desk-only). Concurrent release vs send-back decides once (409 for the loser); two parties approving together still reach approved; a risky revision racing the last approval never leaves the request approved with a held draft. Backend `RentAgreementDraftApprovalTest` (`heldRevisionCannotBeApproved`, `staffPartyCannotSeeAHeldDraft`, `sentBackRevisionStaysHidden`), `RentAgreementMakerCheckerRaceTest` | service-queues | ops/registration-check ("a high-rent draft waits for a second operator…") | ✅ |
| **Police intimation** (LEG-2, `PoliceIntimation`). After the registered copy, the tracker links the Pune City / Pimpri-Chinchwad Police portals and the step stays pending until ops records the owner's confirmation (optional reference and date); then the customer's read shows it done. Backend `RentAgreementPoliceIntimationTest`. | service-queues | ops/registration-check ("staff records police intimation confirmation…") | ✅ |
| **Company, NRI and foreign parties** (LEG-1/LEG-3). A company or firm party is routed to the legal-desk quote flow. A foreign tenant is asked for a passport and visa/OCI instead of Aadhaar, sees the SRO-visit copy and the Form C (24h, indiafrro.gov.in) notice. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement ("company or firm parties…", "a foreign tenant uses passport and visa details…") | ✅ |
| **Identity numbers after a refresh** (UX-7, D-g). Autosave keeps only markers; a restored draft shows a note naming the PAN/Aadhaar fields to re-enter, and no number is in storage. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement ("a restored draft names the identity fields…") | ✅ |
| **One invitation per tenant** (UX-6, D-j). Two tenant rows get two separate invitations (`party_index`); each invitee fills only their own row. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement-cofill ("the requester can address two tenant rows…") | ✅ |
| **`/staff/refunds` and `/admin/refunds` — the Refunds desk.** Tabs Pending / Approved / Rejected / Automatic / Disputes with counts that equal the API's; risk-signal chips only on pending rows; Approve opens a confirm modal (Escape cancels, confirm approves, a repeat is 409); Reject needs a reason (blank disabled, server 422) and restores the entitlement. Staff without the `refunds` function and a customer are refused (403, and kept off the desk). **Read-only is simulated** by stripping `refunds:write` from the `/auth/me` and `/me/bootstrap` payloads, because the seeded roles bundle read and write in one function. Automatic and Disputes are covered by counts only | settings-team-staff | ops/refunds-desk | ✅ |

## Notes on what this suite deliberately cannot reach

**What `i18n.spec.js` covers.** The app is English-only; i18next stays as the string
catalogue and `npm run check:i18n` proves every static `t()` key resolves. The spec covers what a
static scan cannot see: a key whose route namespace never loaded renders as a raw dotted path;
helpers that return `{ key, count }` render `[object Object]` at a call site that forgets to
translate; and a `dzLang` left in storage by the multilingual build must not switch anything.

**Stored ids stay English while labels translate.** Review categories, document categories, finance
categories and ownership basis are persisted on user records, so translating an id would orphan every
row already saved against the old value — a data-migration bug wearing a copy-change costume. The
module probes in that spec (`page.evaluate(() => import('/src/lib/...'))`) are unit assertions in an
e2e costume and exist only to pin that id/label split. Two of the modules they reach into
(`lib/data/finances.js`, `lib/qualityScore.js`) have no http provider yet and are rewritten in P5c;
those assertions then move to wherever the ids come from, so leaving them here gives P5c a
compile-time reminder rather than a silent coverage loss.

**`waitForLoadState('networkidle')` is not an app-readiness signal, and the tap-target sweep proved
it.** Vite fetches the whole module graph and only then evaluates it, so on a cold route the last
response lands about a second before `main.jsx` runs — measured here: last module at +251ms,
`networkidle` at +839ms with `document.body.innerText.length === 0`, `main.jsx` at +1181ms, first
render at +1702ms. The sweep ran against an empty document, found zero elements and asserted
`[] === []`, reporting a pass for a screen it never saw. It is also unreachable after a client-side
route change: a property detail page reached by clicking a card never produced 500ms of network
silence inside 20s, while the same route reached by `goto()` passed.

`tests/mobile/tap-targets.spec.js:renderedSweep` replaced it with four gates: `data-dz-boot="ready"`
set by `main.jsx`; a candidate-count floor (`MIN_CANDIDATES` 5, below the sparsest real screen at 7,
far above the zero an unrendered document scores), re-asserted at measurement time so a route that
renders and discards its subtree cannot slip through; two consecutive equal count readings, which asks
directly whether anything is still arriving and cannot be defeated by a held-open request; and the
absence of `<vite-error-overlay>`, because the app shell alone clears the floor, so a broken route
looks exactly like a sparse one from a count. Keep the two polls separate — folded together, a real
violation reports under the "never rendered" message, which names the wrong cause.

**A lazy-loading defect in a specification-backed read is unreachable from the Java suite.**
`GET /admin/properties` shipped a `LazyInitializationException` on `owner` — the moderation queue
returned 500 for every caller — while `PropertyModerationQueueTest` stayed green. It is not that the
Java test was thin: it already covers status widening, `archived`, `recheck`, `q`, the 403 for a
seeker and the 401 for an anonymous caller. It is that `AbstractApiTest` is `@Transactional`, so the
persistence context stays open for the whole test method and a lazy proxy can always resolve. An
assertion on `content[0].owner.mobile` would pass with *and* without the fix, which is worse than no
test at all. Reproducing it needs a non-transactional test that commits users and properties, and
`draazy_test` is required to stay empty of domain data. So the guard is the live spec — the
moderation-queue test in `property-integration` — and the thing it protects is the join fetch in
`PropertySpecs.adminSearch`, which is written the awkward way (`root.fetch` guarded on
`!Long.class.equals(query.getResultType())`) because Spring Data issues a separate COUNT query where
a join fetch is invalid SQL. Any future `JpaSpecificationExecutor` read that renders an association
is in the same blind spot and belongs here rather than in the Java suite.

**Only the *settlement* half of the paid rent-agreement path is outside this suite.** `POST /service-requests`
prices the request, parks it at `awaiting-payment` and returns a `paymentSessionId` — and because
`PaymentGateway` mints `mock_session_*` in dev and `lib/cashfree.js` returns early on that prefix, the
checkout SDK never opens and the whole journey completes in-browser. So `consumer/services/rent-agreement`
owns the create, the pricing, the park, the honest "payment outstanding" ending and the 409 on a
second unpaid request. What remains backend-only is what a browser genuinely cannot reach:
`ServiceRequestFlowTest.PaidGate` drives the real state machine — the ops queue does not show a
parked request, a signature-verified webhook moves it to `new` (or cancels it on failure),
redelivery is inert, an unrecognised `type` is a 400 rather than a free desk, and identity numbers
in `details` are refused. Confirming that money actually moves needs the Cashfree sandbox, which is
wired up and obtainable by hand on the deployed service — but not from here: this lane runs against a
local backend no callback can reach, signed with a key the suite must not hold.

**Two `no-gate` files divide by surface, not by duplication.** `list-property/no-gate` owns the
wizard interior an owner lands in, while `flatmates/badge-not-gate` owns the flatmates board's
three supply entry points (group form, room hand-off, seeker request) and the claim that granting the
badge changes none of them. The rest of ADR-019's badge-not-gate claim is spread over
`consumer/property/contact-badge-not-gate`, `consumer/flatmates/seeker-verify` (opt-in seeker badge),
`platform/auth/kyc-growth-levers` (the opt-in badge funnel on the dashboard) and `platform/auth/verify-payoff`
(what the badge actually buys).

**Half the verify payoff is backend-only.** A browser cannot earn a badge at all — the grant is a staff
review decision — so the write onto an owner's listings is proven by `VerifiedOwnerListingsTest`
and only the buyer-visible payoff stays in Playwright. Verification now earns seven days of Featured
placement and nothing else buys it: the reward is derived on read from `ownership_verified_at`, so
`consumer/home/featured-reward` pins the clock edges and `property-ownership-review` the real grant and
withdrawal, while `PropertiesEndpointsTest` and `PropertyModerationSummaryTest` pin it in Java,
and `property-integration` asserts no console toggle or `toggle-featured` route survives.

**Legacy routes still have owners.** `/share-flat` redirects, and `consumer/flatmates/discovery`
asserts the legacy `?view=` deep links resolve; it runs cross-viewport because the discovery surface
differs on a phone. Shared fixtures live in `helpers/app.js` (`seed()`, `OWNER`, `SEEKER`, `ADMIN`,
listing factories), and `playwright.config.js` routes viewports on `tests/mobile/**` rather than on a
filename prefix, so the convention is visible in the layout instead of being a rule people must know.

---

## Gap backlog

Debt wave 10 shipped four user-visible frontend changes and **no e2e**, which is the
only open backlog. Each is covered by a backend test or a build-time gate — the gap is
specifically that nothing drives them through a browser, which is where each one would
actually be wrong.

| Open gap | Why it is a gap, not a nicety |
|---|---|
| **Progressive disclosure on `/property/:id`** (D141) — four sections now start collapsed on mobile | `mobile/property-contact` scrolls to a fixed y-offset on this page and passes because the sticky bar is fixed, not because the content below it is right. A collapse that fails open costs nothing; one that fails **shut** silently hides amenities, and no assertion would notice. Needs a spec that expands each of the four and asserts the content arrives. |
| **The Turnstile widget on `/signin` and `/signup`** (D130) | The widget renders only when a site key is configured, so in mock mode it renders nothing and every existing auth spec passes either way. The failure worth catching is the opposite one: a misconfigured key rendering a *blocking* widget on a form whose button is deliberately not gated. |
| **Report triage enforcement** (D68) — `actioned` + `hide_content` must take the listing down | Covered by `ReportService` tests server-side. What no test covers is the admin screen's half: that the enforcement control is *present*, that it is refused on a non-`actioned` decision, and that the dashboard's `openReports` tile agrees with the queue it links to. |
| **`buy.stamp_duty` renders as absent, not as ₹0** (D118 / V55) | `check:finance` proves the client's window matches the server's; it says nothing about what a null renders as. The whole point of the change was that a wrong number looked like a right one. |

### Closed, with what closing them found

The Phase-2 gaps and the seven items that followed them are closed. The table below
records what closing the last batch found, because each one is a trap the next spec
author can fall into again.

| Closed item | What the new spec found |
|---|---|
| `/services` hub → `consumer/services/services-hub` | Nothing broken, but the hub's `.reveal` blocks sit at `opacity: 0` until scrolled into view. `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` deadlocks on them (scrolling requires visibility); force `.visible` instead, as `consumer/property/detail-improvements` does. |
| `/locality/:slug` → `consumer/search/locality-intel` | Stats exist only with at least 3 live listings, so the busy-locality test looks one up at run time and skips when none exists. A freshly minted locality (`mintLocality`, placeId `mint-<slug>`) is the reliable "pending" case. The route is `/locality/:slug`, not `/localities/`. |
| `/pay-rent` → `pay-rent.spec.js` *(retired D256)* | Historical: `draazyDB_v5` could not be seeded in an init script — mockApi migrated and merged it at module load, so a partial object left the app with no settings and a blank page. You loaded once, mutated the real DB, then navigated. The store is gone; the trap cannot recur. |
| `consumer/account/live-rent-payment-seam.spec.js` *(retired with V127)* | Covered the tenant→owner rent rail: `POST /me/rent-payments` opening a Cashfree order, the payment-method vocabulary the server would accept, `/me/rent-ledger`, `/me/rent-mandate` and `/me/payout-account`. All four endpoints and their three tables were dropped, so there is nothing left to assert — not a gap. The one lesson worth carrying forward is why the method assertion was shaped as "not 400 plus the exact `method must be one of` message" rather than 201: the partial unique index answered the *second* valid method in a run with 409, so a 201 assertion would have failed on a correct implementation. Any future rail will need the same shape. |
| `/admin/localities` → `localities.spec.js` *(retired D256)* | `Table` renders a desktop `<table>` **and** a `.dz-card` list for phones, hiding one with CSS. Unscoped text assertions either trip strict mode or resolve to the hidden copy — **still true, and the one lesson here that outlived the mock**. The rest is historical: the queue was **empty** in the seeded store — every listing in `db.json` had a locality — so a test needed one unfiled, and `draazyDB_v5` could not be written in an init script (mockApi migrated and merged it at module load), so you navigated first and mutated after. Both stores are gone. |
| Help feedback → `platform/help/article-feedback` | Fine as shipped on the client branches. Adding the server write found two: the widget reset ran in an effect keyed on the slug, so the first paint of a new article showed the *previous* one's thank-you card with the previous slug in its support link (fixed by keying the component, and the spec now navigates by **click** — the original `goto` remounts and passes either way); and `useHelpLang()` returned i18next's raw `language`, which keeps a region tag (`en-US`) while resources resolve to `en`, so every affected browser's POST would have been a 422 swallowed by the fire-and-forget catch. Normalised at the hook, which fixes every future consumer of it. |
| `DualRange` parsing → `consumer/search/dual-range-parsing` | Fine as shipped: `Cr`/`L`/`Lakh`/`Lac`/`K`, `₹`, commas and spaces all parse; unparseable input is ignored rather than coerced to 0. |
| Responsive pickers → `platform/date-time-fields`, `mobile/date-time-fields` | **Two real bugs.** (1) `.dz-datefield` / `.dz-cal` / `.dz-timepicker` lived in `styles/routes/list-property.css`, which only `ListProperty.jsx` imports — so on the other ~19 surfaces the field rendered as a plain block and the calendar as a static, unlayered element that reflowed the page. Moved to `styles/components/date-time-fields.css`, imported by `DateField.jsx` and `TimeField.jsx`. (2) `.dz-timepicker { width: 250px }` sat after the bottom-sheet block at equal specificity, so on a phone the time picker docked as a 250px stub against the left edge while the calendar went full width. Now guarded by `@media (min-width: 640px)`. |

**Measuring a fixed overlay.** `boundingBox()` is document-relative, so on a
scrolled page it reports a docked sheet hundreds of pixels below the fold and
fails against correct CSS. Read `getBoundingClientRect()` in `evaluate()`, and
poll rather than sample once — the sheet animates up from `translateY(100%)`.

## Known-failing (pre-existing, product/spec drift)

**`consumer/connectivity.spec.js:145`** — *"a request that never reaches the server hedges"*, flaky
(~1 run in 3), and the flakiness is the product's rather than the spec's. `useConnectivity` holds one
global `unreachable` latch that **any** successful request clears, including calls the failing screen
never made, so the banner can flip from "Can't reach the server." to "Back online…" while the page is
still unable to load. Whether the assertion sees the right one is decided by request ordering.

Worth reading before dismissing it as noise: retiring the `lib/store.js` barrel moved the failure rate
from **0/14 to 3/9** without changing a single line of behaviour — nothing imported the deleted
modules; only Vite's chunking, and therefore request ordering, changed. Measured by running the spec N
times either side of a `git stash`, which is the only way to tell a new flake from an old one. The
sibling at `:184` fails at the same rate on **both** sides and is genuinely pre-existing.

Not patched here on purpose — widening the route interception or adding a retry would make the spec
green while leaving the defect it found in place. Tracked as **D218**; the fix belongs in
`useConnectivity`.

No other known-failing specs in this area. Three assertions in the old mock `types` spec under
`consumer/list-property` had drifted from the wizard and were carried here as
known-failing. All three were resolved when the folder moved to the live suite: the
land case now asserts what the product actually does (the 7/12 Extract is the
ownership proof on offer, and the document block raises no error — the old spec
demanded a mandatory-document error the wizard stopped emitting), while the MPCB and
Agreement Duration cases turned out to be reachable and pass unchanged in
`consumer/list-property/types`. The whole folder runs green against the live API.

Each ✅ spec asserts primary buttons/functions (happy path), the role/flag/team **guard**
(redirect when unauthorized), an **empty state**, and any **maker-checker** transition.
